\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\mathcal{S},j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}\,2^{j\alpha}\|\widetilde{P}_{j}g\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

For high frequencies
j\geq j_{\epsilon}
we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{m_{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}}g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{m_{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}}\widetilde{P}_{j}g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\mathcal{S},j}*(\widetilde{P}_{j}g)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\mathcal{S},j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}\,2^{j(1+\alpha)}\|\widetilde{P}_{j}g\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

Combining the above three estimates with the Besov characterization (
53
) of
C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
, we obtain Lemma
1.4
.
∎

3.
Mapping properties of remainder terms

Here we prove Lemma
1.5
regarding mapping properties of the single and double layer potential remainder terms
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
. We additionally show Lemma
1.6
rgarding the angle-averaged single layer operator applied to constant-in-
s
functions.

3.1.
Setup and tools

The proof of Lemma
1.5
relies on a more careful characterization of the layer potential kernels (
22
) and (
23
) along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
as functions of
s
and
\theta
.

We begin in the straight setting. For
\overline{\bm{x}},\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, the difference
\overline{\bm{x}}-\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}
may be written in terms of
s
and
\theta
as

(70)

\overline{\bm{R}}:=\overline{\bm{x}}-\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}=(s-s^{\prime})\bm{e}_{z}+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(\theta^{\prime}))=\textstyle(s-s^{\prime})\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\,.

It will be convenient to work in terms of
\widehat{s}=s-s^{\prime}
and
\widehat{\theta}=\theta-\theta^{\prime}
instead of
s^{\prime}
and
\theta^{\prime}
. We may then write

(71)

\overline{\bm{R}}(\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta})=\textstyle\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,,\quad\widehat{s}=s-s^{\prime}\,,\;\widehat{\theta}=\theta-\theta^{\prime}\,,

so, in particular, using that
\bm{n}_{x}=\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)
and
\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}=\bm{e}_{r}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})
, we have

(72)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x}=-\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,.

In the curved setting, it will again be convenient to consider

(73)

\bm{R}:=\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}

as a function of
(s,\theta)
and
(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
.
Using that
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2,\beta}
and the orthonormal frame ODEs (
3
), we note the following expansions in
s
:

(74)

\displaystyle\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}(-\kappa_{1}(s)\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s))+\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{1}}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}(-\kappa_{2}(s)\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s))+\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{2}}(s,\widehat{s})\,.

Throughout, for any function
Q(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
of both
(s,\theta)
and
(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\in\mathbb{T}\times 2\pi\mathbb{T}
, we will consider the following norms with respect to both pairs of arguments:

(75)

\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{1}}:=\sup_{\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}}\left\lVert Q(\cdot,\cdot,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\,,\quad\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{2}}:=\sup_{s,\theta}\left\lVert Q(s,\theta,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\,.

Recalling the definition (
4
) of
\kappa_{*,\beta}
, the remainder terms
\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})
,
\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{1}}(s,\widehat{s})
, and
\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{2}}(s,\widehat{s})
in (
74
) may be shown to satisfy

(76)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,;\qquad\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}=\widehat{s}Q_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\,,\;\left\lVert Q_{\rm t}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,;

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{{\rm n}_{j}}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{{\rm n}_{j}}(s)=\widehat{s}Q_{{\rm tn}_{j}}(s,\widehat{s})\,,\;\left\lVert Q_{{\rm tn}_{j}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,;

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{{\rm n}_{j}}\cdot\bm{e}_{{\rm n}_{i}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\qquad\mu,i,j\in\{1,2\}\,.

Using (
74
)-(
76
) and recalling the forms (
5
) of
\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
and
\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)
, we also note the following identities and expansions:

(77)

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,,\qquad\textstyle\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)=\textstyle\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)\,,\qquad\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{\theta}(s-\widehat{s},\theta)=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

where
\bm{Q}_{r}
and
\bm{Q}_{\theta}
satisfy

(78)

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\widehat{s}Q_{0,1}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})+\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{0,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})-\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta},\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2},\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\textstyle\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\kappa_{3}\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)^{2}+\kappa_{3}^{2}+\widehat{s}Q_{0,4}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{0,5}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})\,.

Here, using the notation of (
75
), each
Q_{0,j}
satisfies
\left\lVert Q_{0,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})
,
\mu=1,2
. The inverse
\epsilon
-dependence arises from the
\theta
-dependence in these terms, in contrast to (
76
).

Using (
74
) and (
77
), we may expand
\bm{R}
(
73
) as

(79)

\displaystyle\bm{R}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})+\epsilon\big(\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\big)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,.

Furthermore, using the identities in (
78
), we may write

(80)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert^{2}+2\widehat{s}^{3}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{Q}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\big(\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)-2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\big)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-4\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+4\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+2\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{4}\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)-2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{Q}_{\theta}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert^{2}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)+2\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,.

Here the functions
Q_{R,j}
are given by

(81)

\displaystyle Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=-2\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)^{2}+\epsilon\kappa_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{R,1}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=3Q_{\rm t}+\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{R,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2Q_{0,1}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})+4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{0,2}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2},\widehat{s})

\displaystyle+2\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta},\widehat{s})+\epsilon Q_{0,4}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle Q_{R,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 4Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})-4\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2},\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{2}\,.

Note in particular that
Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)
does not depend on
\widehat{s}
and
\widehat{\theta}
while the remaining
Q_{R,j}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{R,1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{R,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\qquad\mu=1,2;\quad j=2,3\,.

Using (
80
), the difference
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}
may be rewritten as

(82)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert}=\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}+\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert)}\,.

Furthermore, for any integer
k\geq 1
, we may expand

(83)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k}}=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k-1-\ell}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}+\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert)}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k-1-\ell}}\,.

We also note that, using that
\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}=\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})
along with the properties (
78
) of
\bm{Q}_{r}
, we have

(84)

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\textstyle\widehat{s}^{2}(\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{e}_{r}+\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}+\epsilon\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{r}\right\rvert^{2})

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,,\qquad\left\lVert Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\;\mu=1,2\,.

Similarly, for
\bm{n}_{x}=\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
, we have

(85)

\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x}=\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm n}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x}+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm n}\,,\quad\left\lVert Q_{\rm n}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\;\mu=1,2\,.

Using the definition (
71
) of
\overline{\bm{R}}
and the expansion (
79
) for
\bm{R}
, we may show the following.

Lemma 3.1

(Relating
\bm{R}
,
\overline{\bm{R}}
, and “flat”)
.

For
\overline{\bm{R}}
,
\bm{R}
as in (
71
), (
79
), the following bounds hold for
\epsilon
sufficiently small:

(86)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert-|\overline{\bm{R}}|\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa_{*}}{2}\widehat{s}^{2}+c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert

(87)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})|\overline{\bm{R}}|\,.

Furthermore,
\overline{\bm{R}}
is close to the “flat” expression
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}
in the following sense:

(88)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert-\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)\epsilon|\widehat{\theta}|^{3}

(89)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq c\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\,.

Proof.

First, noting that

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}=|\overline{\bm{R}}|\,,

the first inequality (
86
) follows from the form (
79
) of
\bm{R}
and the triangle inequality.

Second, using (
86
) and the form (
72
) of
|\overline{\bm{R}}|
, if
\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{*}}
, then

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq|\overline{\bm{R}}|-\frac{\kappa_{*}}{2}\widehat{s}^{2}-c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{6}|\overline{\bm{R}}|+\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert}{2}-\frac{\kappa_{*}}{2}\widehat{s}^{2}+\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert}{3}-c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{6}|\overline{\bm{R}}|

for
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{3c(\kappa_{*})}
. If
\kappa_{*}\leq 2
we are done, since
\max\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert=\frac{1}{2}
. If not, suppose
\frac{1}{\kappa_{*}}\leq\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then, using (
74
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\textstyle\left\lvert\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})\right\rvert-2\epsilon\left\lvert\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\right\rvert\geq c_{\Gamma}\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert-2\epsilon\geq\frac{c_{\Gamma}}{\kappa_{*}}-2\epsilon\geq\frac{c_{\Gamma}}{2\kappa_{*}}

as long as
\epsilon\leq\frac{c_{\Gamma}}{4\kappa_{*}}
. Since
|\overline{\bm{R}}|\leq\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert+2\epsilon\left\lvert\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{c_{\Gamma}}{2\kappa_{*}}
, we obtain (
87
).

The bound (
88
) follows immediately from the triangle inequality and the fact that
|\widehat{\theta}|\leq\pi
and

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{\theta}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)|\widehat{\theta}|^{3}\,.

The final bound (
89
) may be seen using (
88
) as follows. If
(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)\widehat{\theta}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}-(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)\epsilon|\widehat{\theta}|^{3}\geq\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}-\frac{\epsilon}{2}|\widehat{\theta}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\,.

If instead
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)}}\leq|\widehat{\theta}|\leq\pi
, then

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\textstyle\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}\big(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2(\sinh(\pi)-\pi)}}\big)}\geq c\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\pi^{2}}\,.

The above two bounds together yield (
89
).
∎

With the aid of Lemma
3.1
, we may obtain bounds on the following integrals.

Lemma 3.2

(Integral bounds for
\bm{R}
and
\overline{\bm{R}}
)
.

Let
\bm{R}
,
\overline{\bm{R}}
be as in (
79
), (
71
), respectively. Given
0\leq\alpha<1
and an integer
k
satisfying
k-\alpha<2
, we have

(90)

\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{k-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert^{k-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\;\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{2-k+\alpha}&\text{for }1<k-\alpha<2\\
c\,\epsilon&\text{for }k-\alpha\leq 1\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

We start by noting that the following “flat” estimate holds:

(91)

\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+(\epsilon\widehat{\theta})^{2}}^{\,k-\alpha}}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{2-k+\alpha}&\text{for }1<k-\alpha<2\\
c\,\epsilon&\text{for }k\leq 1\,.\end{cases}

This may be seen by rescaling
\widehat{\theta}
by
\epsilon^{-1}
and writing

\displaystyle\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi\epsilon}^{\pi\epsilon}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\widehat{\theta}^{2}}^{\,k-\alpha}}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}=J_{1}+J_{2}\,,

where

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{-2\pi\epsilon}^{2\pi\epsilon}\int_{-\pi\epsilon}^{\pi\epsilon}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\widehat{\theta}^{2}}^{\,k-\alpha}}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\rho\leq 3\pi\epsilon}\frac{1}{\rho^{k-\alpha}}\,\rho d\rho d\phi\leq c\,\epsilon^{2-k+\alpha}

and

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle:=2\int_{2\pi\epsilon}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi\epsilon}^{\pi\epsilon}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\widehat{\theta}^{2}}^{\,k-\alpha}}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

can be directly integrated in
\widehat{\theta}
to yield

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=4\pi\epsilon\int_{2\pi\epsilon}^{1/2}\frac{(\widehat{s}^{2}+\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{2-k+\alpha}{2}}}{\widehat{s}^{2}}\;_{2}F_{1}\big(1,\frac{3-k+\alpha}{2},\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\widehat{s}^{2}}\big)\,d\widehat{s}\,.

Here
\,{}_{2}F_{1}
is the hypergeometric function which, since
\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\widehat{s}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, is defined by the power series

\;{}_{2}F_{1}\big(1,\frac{3-k+\alpha}{2},\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\widehat{s}^{2}}\big)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(k,\alpha)\bigg(-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\widehat{s}^{2}}\bigg)^{n}\,,

where
a_{n}(k,\alpha)
depends on the first three arguments.
Using this expression for
J_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\epsilon\int_{2\pi\epsilon}^{1/2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left\lvert a_{n}\right\rvert(\pi\epsilon)^{2n}\big(\widehat{s}^{\,-2n-k+\alpha}+(\pi\epsilon)^{2-k+\alpha}\widehat{s}^{\,-2n-2}\big)\,d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}(\pi\epsilon)^{2n}\big(\widehat{s}^{\,-2n-k+1+\alpha}+(\pi\epsilon)^{2-k+\alpha}\widehat{s}^{\,-2n-1}\big)\bigg|_{\widehat{s}=2\pi\epsilon}^{\widehat{s}=\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon(1+\epsilon^{1-k+\alpha})\,.

Together, the estimates for
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
yield (
91
).
Combining Lemma
3.1
and the flat estimate (
91
), we then obtain Lemma
3.2
.
∎

Note that the difference (
80
) between
\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}
and
|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{2}
involves two quadratic terms,
\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)
and
\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})
, leading to two terms in the expansion (
82
) with a
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}
singularity. In particular, this difference is small in
\epsilon
but does not gain regularity. We thus define an intermediary term
\bm{R}_{\rm even}
satisfying

(92)

\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{2}:=\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert^{2}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)+\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})\,.

From the form (
72
) of
|\overline{\bm{R}}|
, we note that
|\overline{\bm{R}}|
is even about both
\widehat{s}=0
and
\widehat{\theta}=0
. Since the coefficients
Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)
and
\kappa_{3}
in (
92
) are independent of
\widehat{s}
and
\widehat{\theta}
, we have that, for nonnegative integers
n
and
m
, the expression

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{s}^{n}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{m}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n+m+2}}\,,\quad n+m\text{ odd},

is globally odd about
\widehat{s}=\widehat{\theta}=0
. In particular, we have

(93)

{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{n}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{m}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n+m+2}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}=0\,,

where
{\rm p.v.}
denotes the Cauchy principal value, i.e.

\displaystyle{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}g(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\bigg(\int_{-1/2}^{-\delta}+\int_{\delta}^{1/2}\bigg)\bigg(\int_{-\pi}^{-2\pi\delta}+\int_{2\pi\delta}^{\pi}\bigg)g(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

In addition, we have

(94)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{2}+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert)}\,,

and, by a similar argument to Lemma
3.1
,

(95)

\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert\geq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})|\overline{\bm{R}}|\,.

The expansion (
94
) may be used to show the following bound which relies on near-cancellation in
\widehat{s}
and
\widehat{\theta}
upon integration.

Lemma 3.3

(Bound for integrands with odd powers)
.

Let
\overline{\bm{R}}
,
\bm{R}
be as in (
71
), (
79
) and consider nonnegative integers
\ell,k,n,m
such that
\ell+k+2=n+m
and
\ell+k
is odd. Given
g,\varphi\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

(96)

\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

Proof.

Recalling the definition (
92
) of
\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert
and using the cancellation property (
93
), we may write

\displaystyle{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\,,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle={\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\big(g(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})-g(0,0)\big)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle={\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(0,0)}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n}}\bigg)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle={\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(0,0)}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n}}\big(\varphi(s,\theta)-\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\big)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

Since
g\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{\ell+k+2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

by Lemma
3.2
. To bound
J_{2}
, we use (
94
) to write

(97)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert}\bigg)\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{j}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n-1-j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert)}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{j}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n-1-j}}\,.

Using (
95
), we may also estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}^{4}+\epsilon\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{3}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert)}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{j}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n-1-j}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(1+\frac{\epsilon}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Finally, using that
\varphi\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{3}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{\ell+k+2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Combining the estimates for
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
, and
J_{3}
, we obtain Lemma
3.3
.
∎

We will next need a lemma for estimating the
\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}
seminorm of common terms appearing in the expanded boundary integral expressions.

Lemma 3.4

(Estimating
\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}
seminorms)
.

Let
\ell,k,m,n
be nonnegative integers satisfying either

(1)

\ell+k=m+n-1
or

(2)

\ell+k=m+n-2
and
\ell+k
is odd.

Given
g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\in C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}(\Gamma_{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{\epsilon})
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}>\alpha>0
, for
\overline{\bm{R}}
and
\bm{R}
as in (
71
), (
79
), let
K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
denote

(98)

K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}):=\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert^{n}}\,.

Suppose
\varphi(s,\theta)\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
. Then for any
(s_{0},\theta_{0})\in[-1/2,1/2]\times[-\pi,\pi]
with
s_{0}^{2}+\theta_{0}^{2}\neq 0
we have

(99)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\rm p.v.}\int_{-s_{0}-1/2}^{-s_{0}+1/2}\int_{-\theta_{0}-\pi}^{-\theta_{0}+\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle-{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}&\text{in case (1)}\\
c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\big(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}&\text{in case (2)}\,,\end{cases}

where
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}
and
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}
are as defined in (
75
).

Note that Lemma
3.4
gives us a bound for the
\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}
seminorm for boundary integral expressions with kernels of the form (
98
).

Proof.

Throughout, we will use that the following sequence of inequalities holds due to Lemma
3.1
:

(100)

\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert\geq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})|\overline{\bm{R}}|\geq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\,.

We begin by considering the case
\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\geq\epsilon
. Then, using Lemma
3.2
in case (1) and Lemma
3.3
in case (2), we have

(101)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\rm p.v.}\int_{-s_{0}-1/2}^{-s_{0}+1/2}\int_{-\theta_{0}-\pi}^{-\theta_{0}+\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle-{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-s_{0}-1/2}^{-s_{0}+1/2}\int_{-\theta_{0}-\pi}^{-\theta_{0}+\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle+\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\\
c\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\end{cases}\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}&\text{in case (1)}\\
c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}&\text{in case (2)}.\end{cases}

When
\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}<\epsilon
, we split the integral (
99
) into two regions. First, consider the region
I_{1}
where
\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}\leq 4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
. Then
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}
belongs to the region
I_{1}^{\prime}
where
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\leq 5\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
. In case (1), using the notation

(102)

\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}:=\overline{\bm{R}}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,,

we may use (
100
) to estimate

(103)

\displaystyle\bigg|\iint_{I_{1}}K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle-\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\iint_{\rho\leq 4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\frac{1}{\rho}\,\rho d\rho d\phi+\iint_{\rho\leq 5\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\frac{1}{\rho}\,\rho d\rho d\phi\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,.

In the third inequality we have switched to polar coordinates
(\rho,\phi)
as in the proof of Lemma
3.2
.

In case (2), as in the proof of Lemma
3.3
, we make use of the decomposition

(104)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}={\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\big(K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3}\big)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,,

\displaystyle K_{1}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})=\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\big(g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-g(s,\theta,s,\theta)\big)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle K_{2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})=\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(s,\theta,s,\theta)}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n}}\bigg)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle K_{3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})=\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}g(s,\theta,s,\theta)}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{n}}\big(\varphi(s,\theta)-\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\big)\,.

Over the region
I_{1}^{\prime}
, using that

\displaystyle{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\left\lvert K_{1}\right\rvert+\left\lvert K_{2}\right\rvert+\left\lvert K_{3}\right\rvert\big)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,,

we may follow the same steps (
103
) as in case (1) to obtain

(105)

\displaystyle\bigg|\iint_{I_{1}}K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle-\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,.

Finally, we consider the region
I_{2}
where
\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}>4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
, so
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}>3\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
.
In case (1), making use of (
100
) along with the fact that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}\leq\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\,,

it may be shown that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{K}|

\displaystyle:=\left\lvert K_{\ell kmn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})-K_{\ell kmn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\left\lvert\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{m}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}}\right\rvert\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}+\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{n}}+\left\lvert(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{\ell}-\widehat{s}^{\ell}\right\rvert\frac{|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lvert\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{n}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\right\rvert\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}+\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}}+\left\lvert\textstyle(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}-(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}\right\rvert\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lvert g(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{k}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{m}\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert}+c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}\,,

where we again use the notation (
102
). Using Lemma
3.2
, we then have

(106)

\displaystyle\iint_{I_{2}}|\widetilde{K}|\left\lvert\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\bigg(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\big(\epsilon\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}+\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\,.

In case (2), using the decomposition (
104
), for each
i\in\{1,2,3\}
we may define

\displaystyle|\widetilde{K}_{i}|

\displaystyle:=\left\lvert K_{i}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})-K_{i}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert\,.

Similar to case (1), within the region
I_{2}
it may be shown that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{K}_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle|\widetilde{K}_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{K}_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{3-\alpha}}\bigg)\,.

We may bound
|\widetilde{K}_{2}|
exactly as in (
106
) to obtain

(107)

\iint_{I_{2}}|\widetilde{K}_{2}|\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}+c\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,.

For
|\widetilde{K}_{1}|
and
|\widetilde{K}_{3}|
, using the inequality (
100
) along with a switch to polar coordinates, we obtain

(108)

\displaystyle\iint_{I_{2}}\big(|\widetilde{K}_{1}|+|\widetilde{K}_{3}|\big)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{2}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle+c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{1}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{\,2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{3-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\iint_{\rho>4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\rho^{2-\alpha}}+\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}{\rho^{3-\alpha}}\bigg)\rho d\rho d\phi

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\big(1+|\log(s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2})|\big)+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\big(1+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\big(1+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha-1}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,,

where we have used that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}>\alpha
to absorb the logarithmic term.

Combining the estimates (
101
), (
103
), (
105
), (
106
), (
107
), and (
108
), we obtain Lemma
3.4
.
∎

3.2.
Mapping properties of single layer remainder terms

Equipped with the lemmas of section
3.1
, we may now prove Lemma
1.5
regarding mapping properties of the single and double layer remainder terms
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
. We begin with the single layer remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
, defined in (
33
). Recalling the mapping properties of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
(see Lemma
1.3
), we show that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
may be decomposed into two operators: one which gains more regularity than
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
, and one which is smaller in
\epsilon
. We restate the result (
38
) here for convenience.

Lemma 3.5

(Mapping properties of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
, restated)
.

For
0<\alpha<\gamma\leq\beta<1
, let
\bm{X}\in C^{2,\beta}
be as in section
1.1
and consider
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
as in (
33
). Given
\varphi\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
with
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\varphi(s,\theta)\,ds=0
, we have that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
may be decomposed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[\varphi]+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\varphi]

where

(109)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[\varphi]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\varphi]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Proof.

In order to separate
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
into a piece that is very small in
\epsilon
and a piece that gives additional smoothing, we will need to define an intermediate kernel similar to what was done with
\bm{R}_{\rm even}
in (
92
). We define

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta})=\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta}))\,,

and note that

(110)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert^{2}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,0}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+2\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{S,1}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{S,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{2}+\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{S,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{S,4}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

where, using the notation of section
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{S,0}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-2\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)^{2}+\epsilon\kappa_{3}^{2}-4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})

\displaystyle Q_{S,1}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{0,1}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{0,4}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle Q_{S,2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 4Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})-4\bm{Q}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)+4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{\theta}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{S,3}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{Q}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{S,4}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-4\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,.

Here, using the notation (
75
), each
Q_{S,j}
satisfies the
C^{0,\beta}
estimates

(111)

\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}\,c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\quad\mu=1,2\,.

Note, in particular, that the difference between
\bm{R}_{\rm t}
and
\bm{R}
is higher order in
\widehat{s}
, while the difference between
\bm{R}_{\rm t}
and
\overline{\bm{R}}
is higher order in
\epsilon
. As in (
82
) and (
83
), we have that the inverse differences
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{k}}
and
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k}}
may be written

(112)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{S,3}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{S,4}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert)}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{\ell}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{k-1-\ell}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,0}+2\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{S,1}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{S,2}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert|\overline{\bm{R}}|(\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert+|\overline{\bm{R}}|)}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k-1-\ell}}\,.

Furthermore, we have that
\bm{R}_{\rm t}
satisfies the same estimates as
\bm{R}
in each of Lemmas
3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
, and
3.4
by analogous arguments.

Using the representation (
47
) of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
and recalling the form (
7
) of the Jacobian factor
\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})
, we proceed by considering the single layer remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
in four parts:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi](s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}[\varphi](s,\theta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}[\varphi](s,\theta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}[\varphi](s,\theta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}[\varphi](s,\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-1/2}+\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\bigg)\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

We begin by bounding the smooth remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}
away from the singularity at
\widehat{s}=\widehat{\theta}=0
. Since
\int_{0}^{1}\varphi(s,\theta)\,ds=0
, we may write
\varphi(s,\theta)=\partial_{s}\Phi(s,\theta)
for some
\Phi
with
\Phi(0,\theta)=0
. Then, integrating by parts in
\widehat{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-1/2}+\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\bigg)\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{\widehat{s}}\bigg(\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\Phi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\,\Phi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}\bigg|_{\widehat{s}=-1/2}^{\widehat{s}=1/2}\,.

In particular,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\widehat{s}^{2}}d\widehat{s}+c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\int_{0}^{s}\varphi\,ds\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},0}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{1}{\widehat{s}^{3}}+\frac{\epsilon}{\widehat{s}^{4}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\widehat{s}^{5}}\bigg)d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Moving on to the remainders
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}
, we may use (
112
) and Lemma
3.2
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{3}\left\lvert Q_{S,3}\right\rvert+\widehat{s}^{2}|\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|\left\lvert Q_{S,4}\right\rvert}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert)}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert|\sin(\widehat{\theta})|}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert|\overline{\bm{R}}|(\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert+|\overline{\bm{R}}|)}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon^{2}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Using Lemma
3.4
, case (1), we may similarly estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next bound derivatives of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},j}
,
j=1,2,3
, in
s
and
\theta
. We begin with
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
and note that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta))\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\widehat{s}(\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s)+\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s))+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

while

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta))\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\,.

We may then write

(113)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}}+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{S,5}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})-\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,6}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

\displaystyle Q_{S,5}

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta})-(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa})\big(\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)-\widehat{s}Q_{0,1}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)\big)+\epsilon\kappa_{3}^{2}\cos(\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle Q_{S,6}

\displaystyle=(\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s)+\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s))\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

where
Q_{0,1}
and
\bm{Q}_{r}
are as in section
3.1
. In addition, we have

(114)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,6}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,7}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle Q_{S,7}

\displaystyle=-\big((1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta))\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\big)\cdot\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})

where
Q_{S,6}
is as in (
113
) and
\bm{Q}_{\rm t}
is as in section
3.1
. Each of
Q_{S,5}
,
Q_{S,6}
,
Q_{S,7}
additionally satisfy the bound (
111
).
Using (
113
) and (
114
), we may write

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)+\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}-\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{s}+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{S,5}-\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,6}\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)+\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,6}+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,7}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,,

where we note that
\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}}=\widehat{s}
.

Using (
112
) to expand
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}
and noting the additional power of
\widehat{s}
in the difference, we may apply Lemma
3.2
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, for any
\gamma
satisfying
\alpha<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}<1
, we may use case (1) of Lemma
3.4
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}\bigg(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}_{1}}\bigg)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Similarly, for
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}
we may calculate

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)-\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{S,5}-\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{S,6}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}\,.

Using (
112
) and noting the additional
\epsilon
, we may use Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1+\alpha}\,\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Furthermore, using Lemma
3.4
, case (2), we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Finally, using (
113
) and (
114
), by Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1+\alpha}\,\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\widehat{\kappa}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Similarly, by Lemma
3.4
, case (2), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\widehat{\kappa}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Next we estimate
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Note that
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}_{\rm t}=\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)
. Using the identities of section
3.1
, we may write

(115)

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\kappa_{3}\cos(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}}+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{S,8}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

where
Q_{0,3}
is as in (
78
) and
Q_{S,8}
satisfies the bound (
111
).
We may then write

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}=\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\big(\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\kappa_{3}\cos(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{0,3}\big)\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,.

Again using (
112
) to expand
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}
, we may use Lemma
3.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, again choosing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
to satisfy
\alpha<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}\leq\beta
, we may use case (1) of Lemma
3.4
to estimate

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}\,\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}_{1}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Next, using (
115
), we may write

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)+\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{R}_{\rm t}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)-\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{S,8}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}\,.

We may then use the expansion (
112
) of
\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert^{3}}
along with Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
to estimate

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1+\alpha}\,\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

As was the case for
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}
, we may next use Lemma
3.4
, case (2) to obtain the
\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}
estimate

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm t}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Finally, using (
115
), we may estimate
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}
similarly to
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}
to obtain

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1+\alpha}\,\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\widehat{\kappa}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Again by case (2) of Lemma
3.4
we have

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},3}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{S,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\varphi\widehat{\kappa}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

In total, we obtain Lemma
3.5
.
∎

3.3.
Mapping properties of double layer remainder terms

We next show estimate (
39
) of Lemma
1.5
regarding the double layer remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
defined in (
34
). In particular, we show that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
is smoother than
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}-\overline{{\mathcal{D}}})
and obtain explicit
\epsilon
dependence for the bound. We reiterate the result here for convenience.

Lemma 3.6

(Mapping properties of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
, restated)
.

For
0<\gamma<\beta<1
, let
\bm{X}\in C^{2,\beta}
be as in section
1.1
and consider
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
as in (
34
). Given
\psi\in C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

(116)

\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\psi]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any
\gamma^{+}\in(\gamma,\beta]
.

Proof.

Due to the form (
47
) of
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
used in section
2
and the Jacobian factor (
7
) appearing in (
34
), we consider the double layer remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
in three parts:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\psi](s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},0}[\psi](s,\theta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}[\psi](s,\theta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}[\psi](s,\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},0}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-1/2}+\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\bigg)\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(K_{\mathcal{D}}-\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

We begin with the smooth remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},0}
away from the singularity at
\widehat{s}=\widehat{\theta}=0
. The straight kernel
\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
may be parameterized as

(117)

\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{-2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\,,

and thus, using the form (
70
) of
\overline{\bm{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},0}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1/2}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{3}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{s}^{4}}+\frac{1}{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{5}}\bigg)d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next turn to
L^{\infty}
bounds for the remainders
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}
. Using the expansion (
84
), we have that the curved kernel
K_{\mathcal{D}}
may be expressed as

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{D}}-\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}-\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}+\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\bigg)\,.

We may then use equation (
83
) and Lemma
3.2
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left\lvert K_{\mathcal{D}}-\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}\right\rvert\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\widehat{s}^{2}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}+\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert+\widehat{s}^{4}+\epsilon\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{3}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}|\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}\right\rvert)}\sum_{\ell=0}^{2}\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{2-\ell}}\bigg)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Similarly, using the form (
117
) of
\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
, we may estimate
