\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left\lvert K_{\mathcal{D}}\right\rvert\,\epsilon^{2}d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}+\frac{\epsilon}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\bigg)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next show
C^{0,\gamma}
bounds for
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
. We begin by noting that the straight kernel
\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies

(118)

\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}=-3\frac{\widehat{s}\,\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}=6\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,.

Using that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}=(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta))\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

along with the form (
79
) of
\bm{R}
and the remainder expressions (
78
), we have that

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\partial_{s}\bm{R}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}+\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{\rm s,1}(s,\widehat{s})+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{\rm s,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}\sin(\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})=\widehat{s}\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)-\kappa_{3}\epsilon\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{\rm s,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,.

Here, using the notation (
75
), the functions
Q_{{\rm s},j}
satisfy

(119)

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\quad\mu=1,2

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\quad\mu=1,2\,;\;j=2,3\,.

Altogether, we have that the curved kernel
K_{\mathcal{D}}
may be written

(120)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}K_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=-3\frac{(\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}})(\bm{R}\cdot\partial_{s}\bm{R})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}+\frac{(\partial_{s}\bm{R})\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}+6\epsilon\textstyle\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\bigg)+\frac{\widehat{s}\,\widehat{\kappa}-\kappa_{3}\epsilon\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{\rm s,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}+\frac{6\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{\rm s,1}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle+\frac{6\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}Q_{\rm s,2}+\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{\rm s,4}+\epsilon^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm s,5}+12\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{3}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

where
Q_{\rm s,4}=-3Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}(1+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm s,1}+\epsilon Q_{\rm s,2})
and
Q_{\rm s,5}=-6Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}\kappa_{3}\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})
for
Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})
as in (
84
).

Using equation (
83
) to rewrite
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}
, we may apply Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
along with the estimates (
119
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(\partial_{s}K_{\mathcal{D}}-\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\gamma}\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

We may similarly apply Lemma
3.4
to obtain a
\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}
estimate for
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(\partial_{s}K_{\mathcal{D}}-\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

For
\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}
, we use the form (
118
) of
\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
along with Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{s}K_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\gamma}\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Similarly, using Lemma
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{s}K_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{{\rm s},j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-\beta}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Finally, we prove
C^{0,\gamma}
bounds for
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
. We again begin by calculating the straight kernel
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
, given by

(121)

\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}=\displaystyle-\frac{3}{\epsilon}\frac{(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}})(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}+\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}})\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}_{x^{\prime}}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}=\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}-\frac{2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\,.

Using that

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}=\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

along with the form (
79
) of
\bm{R}
, we may write

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bm{R}\cdot\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\theta,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}Q_{\theta,2}\,,\quad\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}=\textstyle-2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}Q_{\theta,3}\,,

where again, using the notation (
75
), each
Q_{\theta,j}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
satisfies

(122)

\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{\mu}}\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,\quad\mu=1,2\,.

The curved kernel
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}
may then be written

(123)

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=-3\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}})(\bm{R}\cdot\partial_{\theta}\bm{R})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}+\frac{\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\partial_{\theta}\bm{R})\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}+\textstyle 12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\bigg)-\textstyle 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{\widehat{s}Q_{\theta,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-3\frac{2\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}Q_{\theta,2}+\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\theta,4}+\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{\theta,5}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

where
Q_{\theta,4}=2(\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{\theta,1}+\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{\rm n^{\prime}})
and
Q_{\theta,5}=Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}(\epsilon Q_{\theta,2}+\widehat{s}Q_{\theta,1})
for
Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})
as in (
84
).

Again using equation (
83
) to rewrite
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}
and
\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}
, by Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
we may estimate

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}-\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\gamma}\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Furthermore, using Lemma
3.4
and the form (
123
) of
\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}-\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})
, we may obtain the
\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}
estimate

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}-\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}})\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Next, for
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}
, using (
123
) and the form (
121
) of
\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
, by Lemmas
3.3
and
3.2
we obtain

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\gamma}\big(\max_{j}\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Finally, using Lemma
3.4
, we may show

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}K_{\mathcal{D}}\,\psi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\max_{j}\big(\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{\theta,j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}_{2}}\big)\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Combining each of the estimates for
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},0}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},1}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D},2}
, we obtain Lemma
3.6
.
∎

3.4.
Proof of Lemma
1.6

Here we show the decomposition of the
\theta
-averaged single layer operator presented in Lemma
1.6
; in particular, for constant-in-
s
function
h(\theta)
, we show that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathcal{S}[h(\theta)]\,\epsilon\,d\theta=\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}[h(\theta)]+\mathcal{H}_{+}[h(\theta)]

for
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}
which is small in
\epsilon
and
\mathcal{H}_{+}
which is smoother. We begin by letting

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathcal{S}[h(\theta)]\,\epsilon\,d\theta

\displaystyle=H_{1}(s)+H_{2}(s)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathcal{G}\,h(\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon^{2}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}d\theta

\displaystyle H_{2}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathcal{G}\,h(\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon^{3}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}d\theta

where
\mathcal{G}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}
. We may use Lemmas
3.2
and
3.4
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert H_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert H_{2}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{3-\alpha}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next consider
\partial_{s}H_{1}
and
\partial_{s}H_{2}
. We note that for a curved filament,
\mathcal{G}
is not a convolution kernel with respect to
s
, but it nearly is in the following sense. Using the form (
79
) of
\bm{R}
(and noting that we are using
s^{\prime}
rather than
\widehat{s}=s-s^{\prime}
), we may show

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}=-\partial_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{G}+\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\partial_{s}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}-\partial_{s^{\prime}}\bm{R}\cdot\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}=-\partial_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{G}+\frac{\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})Q_{H1}+\epsilon^{2}\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})Q_{H2}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}

where
Q_{H1}
and
Q_{H2}
both satisfy bounds of the form (
111
). Using that
h(\theta^{\prime})
is independent of
s^{\prime}
, we then have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}H_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}(s,s^{\prime},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\,h(\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon^{2}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})Q_{H1}+\epsilon^{2}\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})Q_{H2}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,h(\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon^{2}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}d\theta\,.

By Lemma
3.4
, we then have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}H_{1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}H_{1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\partial_{s}\mathcal{G}\,h(\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon^{3}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}d\theta\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Recall the notation
P_{j}
,
P_{<j}
,
P_{\geq j}
associated with the Littlewood-Paley projection (
52
) and let
j_{\epsilon}=\frac{\left\lvert\log(2\pi\epsilon)\right\rvert}{\log(2)}
as in the proof of Lemma
1.4
.
We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}=\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(P_{\geq j_{\epsilon}}H_{1}+H_{2})\,,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{+}=\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(P_{<j_{\epsilon}}H_{1})\,,

and will use that the high frequencies here are small in
\epsilon
and the low frequencies are smoother.
Using Lemma
1.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c\left\lvert H_{1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}}+c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert H_{2}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+c\,\left\lvert H_{2}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Furthermore, again using Lemma
1.4
, for
\alpha<\gamma\leq\beta
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{+}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert P_{<j_{\epsilon}}H_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+c\left\lvert P_{<j_{\epsilon}}H_{1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert P_{<j_{\epsilon}}H_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+c\,2^{j_{\epsilon}}\left\lvert P_{<j_{\epsilon}}H_{1}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert H_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\gamma}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

∎

4.
Bounds for full Neumann data

Here we prove Lemma
1.7
bounding the full Neumann data
w(s,\theta)
appearing in (
32
) in terms of the
\theta
-independent Dirichlet data
v(s)
. Our approach will rely on a different layer potential representation of the full Dirichlet-to-Neumann map
v=u\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\mapsto w=\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bm{n}_{x}}\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}
for
u
harmonic in
\Omega_{\epsilon}
.

Following
[
26
, Chapter 6]
, given
\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})
,
\bm{x}^{\prime}=\bm{X}(s^{\prime})+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we begin by defining the modified double layer potential

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi](\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\bigg(K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})+\frac{\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert}\bigg)\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\bm{x}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\,,

where
K_{\mathcal{D}}
is as in (
23
),
\bm{X}(s^{\prime})
is the nearest point on the filament centerline to
\bm{x}^{\prime}
, and
dS_{x^{\prime}}
denotes the surface element with respect to
\bm{x}^{\prime}
on
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
. The form of the modified kernel is based on the method used in
[
24
]
for a double layer formulation of the Stokes flow about a slender body and is designed to eliminate the null space of the operator
\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}
along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, which consists of constants.
For
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we will use the same notation to denote

(124)

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi](\bm{x})=\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert}\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,.

Note that for
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
,
\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\epsilon
.
Given Dirichlet data
v(\bm{x})
,
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we have that

(125)

u(\bm{x})=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi](\bm{x})\qquad\text{for }\bm{x}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}

is solution of exterior Dirichlet problem

\displaystyle\Delta u=0\quad\text{in }\Omega_{\epsilon}\,,\qquad u\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=v(\bm{x})

provided that the density
\varphi
on
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
satisfies the integral equation

(126)

v(\bm{x})=\textstyle(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})[\varphi](\bm{x})\,,\qquad\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}\,,

where the
\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}
comes from the exterior jump relation (
24
). This may be seen by noting that if
u(\bm{x})
is given by (
125
) for
\varphi
satisfying
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})[\varphi]=0
on
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, then
u\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=0
and
u(\bm{x})=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi](\bm{x})\sim\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{x}\right\rvert}
as
\left\lvert\bm{x}\right\rvert\to\infty
, so by uniqueness for the exterior Dirichlet problem,
u=0
in
\Omega_{\epsilon}
. Furthermore, by continuity of
\frac{\partial u}{\partial\bm{n}_{x}}
across
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
(see
[
26
, Theorem 6.20]
), we have that
within
the slender body
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
,
u
is harmonic with zero Neumann data. In particular,
u\equiv{\rm constant}
in
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
, and therefore
\varphi\equiv{\rm constant}
on
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
. However, since
\varphi
is constant, using the jump relation
\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}=-\frac{1}{2}
for
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
(see
[
26
, Example 6.17]
), we have
0=(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})[\varphi]=\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{\varphi}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert}\,dS_{x^{\prime}}
, and thus
\varphi=0
due to the correction term.

Along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we denote the normal derivative of the double layer potential by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\varphi](\bm{x})=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{n}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi](\bm{x})\,,\quad\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}\,.

Here again the minus sign arises because
\bm{n}_{x}
points out from the slender body
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
. The operator
\mathcal{T}
is hypersingular and must be understood as a finite part integral
[
42
]
:

(127)

\mathcal{T}[\varphi](\bm{x})={\rm p.v.}\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{T}}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})\big(\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\varphi(\bm{x})\big)\,dS_{x^{\prime}}+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{(\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\cdot\bm{n}_{x}}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert^{3}}\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,,

where the kernel
K_{\mathcal{T}}
is given by

(128)

K_{\mathcal{T}}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{n}_{x}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{G}}{\partial\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\frac{\bm{n}_{x}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\right\rvert^{3}}-3\frac{(\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime})\cdot\bm{n}_{x}\,(\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime})\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\right\rvert^{5}}\bigg)\,.

The representation (
127
) may be obtained by first noting that within the exterior domain
\Omega_{\epsilon}
, the double layer kernel
K_{\mathcal{D}}
integrates to zero (see
[
26
, Example 6.17]
):

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{y},\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}=0\,,\quad\bm{y}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\,.

In particular, inserting a constant-in-
\bm{y}
density
\varphi(\bm{x})
,
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{y}\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{y},\bm{x}^{\prime})\varphi(\bm{x})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}=0\,,\quad\bm{y}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\,,\;\;\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}\,.

We may then consider the first term of
\mathcal{T}
as

\displaystyle-\lim_{\bm{y}\to\bm{x}}\bm{n}_{x}\cdot\nabla_{y}\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}K_{\mathcal{D}}(\bm{y},\bm{x}^{\prime})\big(\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\varphi(\bm{x})\big)\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,,\quad\bm{y}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\,,\;\;\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}\,.

Given
v(\bm{x})
,
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we thus obtain a representation for the full Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) map as

(129)

w(\bm{x})=\textstyle\mathcal{T}(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}[v](\bm{x})\,,\qquad\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}\,,

i.e. as the normal derivative of the solution (
126
) to the exterior Dirichlet problem with data
v(\bm{x})
.

Using the representation (
129
), we proceed to prove Lemma
1.7
in three steps, summarized in the following three lemmas.

Lemma 4.1

(Modified double layer smoothing)
.

Let
0<\gamma<\beta<1
. Given a fiber centerline
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2,\beta}
as in section
1.1
and a surface density
\varphi\in C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, the modified double layer operator
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
given by (
124
) satisfies

(130)

\left\lVert\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any
\gamma^{+}\in(\gamma,\beta]
.

The proof of Lemma
4.1
appears in section
4.1
.
In addition, we show the following uniform bound for
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}
with respect to the filament centerline shape:

Lemma 4.2

(Uniform bounds in
\kappa
)
.

Let
0<\gamma<\beta<1
. Given a fixed fiber radius
\epsilon>0
, for any filament centerline
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2,\beta}
satisfying the non-self-intersection condition (
2
), the inverse
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}
involving the modified double layer is uniformly bounded with respect to the centerline shape; i.e. given
\varphi\in C^{0,\gamma}
, we have

(131)

\left\lVert\textstyle(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any
\gamma^{+}\in(\gamma,\beta]
.

The proof is given via a compactness argument in section
4.2
. Unlike most of the other bounds we obtain, the
\epsilon
-dependence of the constant in (
131
) is not explicit.

The final lemma regards the mapping properties of the hypersingular operator
\mathcal{T}
. In particular, we may extract a leading order piece plus a smoother remainder as follows.

Lemma 4.3

(Hypersingular operator bounds)
.

Let
\bm{X}\in C^{2,\alpha}
be as in section
1.1
and consider
\mathcal{T}
as in (
127
). Given
\varphi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we may decompose
\mathcal{T}[\varphi]
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\varphi]=\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi]+\mathcal{T}_{+}[\varphi]

where

(132)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

The proof of Lemma
4.3
appears in section
4.3
.

We may combine Lemmas
4.1
,
4.2
, and
4.3
to show Lemma
1.7
as follows.

Proof of Lemma
1.7
.

Given
\theta
-independent Dirichlet data
v(s)
in (
126
), we may solve for the intermediate density
\varphi(\bm{x})=\varphi(s,\theta)
as

\displaystyle\varphi(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=2v(s)-\mathcal{M}[v(s)]\,,\qquad\mathcal{M}=\textstyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}\,.

Using Lemmas
4.1
and
4.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},\epsilon)\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Note that we will not make use of the full
C^{1,\alpha}
regularity of
v(s)
because (1) it is not strictly necessary for our main goal of simply showing that certain remainder terms are smoother, since we will just take
\gamma>\alpha
, and (2) it would involve an even nastier computation of derivatives along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
.

We may use Lemma
4.3
to write
w(s,\theta)
from (
129
) as

\displaystyle w(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}[2v(s)]-\mathcal{T}\mathcal{M}[v(s)]

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{T}_{0}[v(s)]+2\mathcal{T}_{+}[v(s)]-\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{M}[v(s)]-\mathcal{T}_{+}\mathcal{M}[v(s)]\,.

Since
v\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{0}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\partial_{s}v\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,,

while each of the other terms are smoother. In particular, for
\alpha<\gamma<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}\leq\beta
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\bigg)\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma},\epsilon)\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\mathcal{M}[v]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma},\epsilon)\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Combining the above bounds, we obtain Lemma
1.7
.
∎

The remainder of this section is devoted to the proofs of Lemmas
4.1
,
4.2
, and
4.3
.

4.1.
Proof of Lemma
4.1
: modified double layer

To show the mapping properties of the modified double layer operator
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
given by (
124
), we begin by writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\varphi]+\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})}{\left\lvert\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\right\rvert}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,,

where
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
is as in (
34
) and satisfies Lemma
1.5
, i.e.

(133)

\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

It thus remains to estimate
\mathcal{D}_{2}
.
Using the representation (
117
) of
\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(c\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+c(\kappa_{*})\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, recalling (
118
) and (
121
), we may calculate

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}=-6\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,\widehat{s}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,,\quad\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{K}_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}-\frac{2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\,.

Then, using Lemma
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}6\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,\widehat{s}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\big|(1+\epsilon\widehat{\kappa})\bm{e}_{\rm t}+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}\big|}{\epsilon^{3}}\,\left\lvert\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\right\rvert\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

as well as

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}-\frac{2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\bigg)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\,\left\lvert\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\right\rvert\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Finally, using case (2) of Lemma
3.4
, we may obtain
\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}
bounds for derivatives of
\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}6\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,\widehat{s}}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad+c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2+\gamma}}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

and

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{D}_{2}[\varphi]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}-\frac{2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{3}}\bigg)\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad+c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2+\gamma}}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Combining the estimates for
\mathcal{D}_{2}
with (
133
), we obtain Lemma
4.1
.
∎

4.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.2
: inverse double layer

Throughout, we consider the surface density
\varphi=\varphi(s,\theta)
along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
as a function of the surface parameters
s
and
\theta
.
Using the uniform boundedness principle
1
1

1

The uniform boundedness principle applies if the underlying Banach space is the same. Technically the space
C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
is different for each curve
\bm{X}(s)
; however, these spaces may be identified across different
\bm{X}(s)
using the norm (
35
).
, it will suffice to fix
\varphi\neq 0
in
C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
and show that

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c_{\varphi}(\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any centerline curve
\bm{X}(s)
satisfying
\left\lVert\kappa\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}}\leq\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}
as well as the non-self-intersection condition (
2
) with the same
c_{\Gamma}
.
A priori
the constant
c_{\varphi}
may depend on the function
\varphi
; however, since
\varphi
is arbitrary, uniform boundedness will imply that the bound in fact holds for a constant that is uniform in
\varphi
.

We proceed by contradiction. Fix
\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
. If no such bound exists, then we may select a sequence of curves
\bm{X}_{j}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
, each satisfying (
2
) and
\left\lVert(\bm{X}_{j})_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}}=\left\lVert\kappa_{j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}}\leq\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}
, such that

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}>j\,,

where
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime}
denotes the modified double layer operator (
124
) on curve
j
.
Defining

\displaystyle h_{j}=\frac{\varphi}{\|(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]\|_{C^{0,\gamma}}}\,,\quad g_{j}=\frac{(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]}{\|(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime})^{-1}[\varphi]\|_{C^{0,\gamma}}}\,,

and noting that
\left\lVert g_{j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
, we have that, by assumption,

(134)

\textstyle(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime})[g_{j}]=h_{j}\to 0\quad\text{in }C^{0,\gamma}\,.

Note that by Lemma
4.1
,
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime}[g_{j}]
is smoother and is bounded in
C^{1,\gamma}
uniformly in
j
. Thus by (
134
), along a subsequence
j_{\ell}
,
g_{j_{\ell}}-2h_{j_{\ell}}
converges strongly in
C^{0,\gamma}
to some limit
g_{\infty}
with
\left\lVert g_{\infty}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
.

Now, since
\left\lVert(\bm{X}_{j})_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}}=\left\lVert\kappa_{j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}}\leq\kappa_{*,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}}
, there exists a subsequence
\bm{X}_{j_{k}}
of curves converging (after a possible translation) strongly in
C^{2,\gamma}
,
0<\gamma<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{+}}
, to some limit curve
\bm{X}_{\infty}(s)
which also satisfies the assumption (
2
). In particular, using the form (
23
) of the double layer kernel
K_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have, for any
\psi\in C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}_{j_{k}}^{\prime}-\mathcal{D}_{\infty}^{\prime})[\psi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{(\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s)-\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s^{\prime}))\cdot\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)\cdot\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})-1)}{\left\lvert\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s)-\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s^{\prime})+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}))\right\rvert^{3}}\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(\bm{X}_{\infty}(s)-\bm{X}_{\infty}(s^{\prime}))\cdot\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)\cdot\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})-1)}{\left\lvert\bm{X}_{\infty}(s)-\bm{X}_{\infty}(s^{\prime})+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}))\right\rvert^{3}}\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\bigg)\,\psi(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{\left\lvert\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s)-\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s^{\prime})+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)\right\rvert}-\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{\left\lvert\bm{X}_{\infty}(s)-\bm{X}_{\infty}(s^{\prime})+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)\right\rvert}\bigg)\psi(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,,

where each of
\bm{e}_{r,j}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j}
,
\widehat{\kappa}_{j}
are defined along the curve
\bm{X}_{j}
. From this we can see that
\left\lvert(\mathcal{D}_{j_{k}}^{\prime}-\mathcal{D}_{\infty}^{\prime})[\psi]\right\rvert\to 0
as
j_{k}\to\infty
. By a diagonalization argument, we therefore have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\prime}g_{j}\to D_{\infty}^{\prime}g_{\infty}

along some subsequence, and
g_{\infty}
satisfies
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{\infty}^{\prime})[g_{\infty}]=0
. By injectivity of the modified double layer (see discussion below (
126
)), we have
g_{\infty}=0
, which contradicts
\left\lVert g_{\infty}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
.
∎

4.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.3
: bounds for hypersingular operator

We begin by writing down a more detailed expression for the kernel
K_{\mathcal{T}}
given by (
128
). Using (
78
), (
84
), and (
85
), we have

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\frac{\bm{n}_{x}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-3\frac{\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x}\,\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}_{x^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\bigg(\frac{1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{0,5}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{4}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+6\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}^{2}(Q_{\rm n}-Q_{\rm n^{\prime}})+3\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{\rm n}Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}\bigg)\,.

We consider the hypersingular part of the operator
\mathcal{T}
in (
127
) in terms of the surface parameters
s
and
\theta
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\varphi]

\displaystyle={\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}K_{\mathcal{T}}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{(\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\cdot\bm{n}_{x}}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert^{3}}\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,.

We may then decompose
\mathcal{T}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi]+\mathcal{T}_{+}[\varphi]\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\,{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{+}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{12\epsilon^{2}\sin^{4}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+6\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\widehat{s}^{2}(Q_{\rm n}-Q_{\rm n^{\prime}})+3\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{\rm n}Q_{\rm n^{\prime}}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{0,5}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{(\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\cdot\bm{n}_{x}}{\left\lvert\bm{x}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert^{3}}\varphi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,.

Here
\mathcal{T}_{0}
captures the main behavior of
\mathcal{T}
while
\mathcal{T}_{+}
is smoother. We begin by estimating
\mathcal{T}_{+}
. Using Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{+}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-1}+1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg)\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Using case (1) of Lemma
3.4
, we may also estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{+}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next turn to bounds for
\mathcal{T}_{0}
. Since
\varphi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we may write

(135)

\displaystyle\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\widehat{s}\,\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)-\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

where

(136)

\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,s}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,\theta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\textstyle c\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

We may then write

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}

\displaystyle=J_{1}+J_{2}\,,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg(\widehat{s}\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)+\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\,\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)\bigg)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\big(\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

We begin with
L^{\infty}
estimates for
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
. First, using Lemma
3.3
, we may estimate
J_{1}
as

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert+c\,\left\lVert\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\displaystyle\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\epsilon\widehat{\theta}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\,.

We may next use Lemma
3.2
to estimate
J_{2}
as

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\big(\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,s}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,\theta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\,.

In total we have

(137)

\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\,.

To prove the
\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}
estimate for
\mathcal{T}_{0}
, we may follow the approach of Lemma
3.4
while being a bit more careful with the structure of remainder terms. We again let
\bm{R}
,
\bm{R}_{0}
denote

\displaystyle\bm{R}=\bm{R}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}),\quad\bm{R}_{0}=\bm{R}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,,

and aim to bound the expression

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi](s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)-\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi](s,\theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-s_{0}-1/2}^{-s_{0}+1/2}\int_{-\theta_{0}-\pi}^{-\theta_{0}+\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

We begin by noting two different expansions for
\varphi
about
(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)
. In particular, we write

(138)

\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)=(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}+\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}\,,

where we will make use of two different forms of the remainder terms. Expansion 1 is given by

(139)

\displaystyle(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}

\displaystyle=-(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\partial_{s}\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert s_{0}+\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,,

while expansion 2 is given by

(140)

\displaystyle(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}

\displaystyle=-(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)+\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)+\left\lvert\epsilon\theta_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,.

Here each
Q_{\varphi,\mu}
is as in (
136
).

Again as in Lemma
3.4
, we first consider the case
\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\geq\epsilon
. Then, using the first expansion (
139
) of the remainder terms, we may use the
L^{\infty}
estimate (
137
) from above to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi](s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}[\varphi](s,\theta)\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\,.

We next consider the case
\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}<\epsilon
.
Using (
127
) and the expansions (
135
) and (
138
), we may write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{0}

\displaystyle=J_{\alpha,{\rm s}}+J_{\alpha,\theta}\,,

\displaystyle J_{\alpha,{\rm s}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2-s_{0}}^{1/2-s_{0}}\int_{-\pi-\theta_{0}}^{\pi-\theta_{0}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\big(-\widehat{s}\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)+\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle J_{\alpha,\theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2-s_{0}}^{1/2-s_{0}}\int_{-\pi-\theta_{0}}^{\pi-\theta_{0}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg(\textstyle-\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\bigg)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

To bound
J_{\alpha,{\rm s}}
and
J_{\alpha,\theta}
, we split the integrals into two regions:

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\big\{(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\;:\;\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}\leq 4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\big\}\,,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\big\{(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\;:\;\sqrt{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})^{2}}>4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\big\}\,.

For
j={\rm s},\theta
, let
J_{\alpha,j,1}
denote the integral of the integrand of
J_{\alpha,j}
over the region
I_{1}
. Note that
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}
then belongs to the region
I_{1}^{\prime}
where
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}\leq 5\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
. Using the first expansion (
139
) for
\varphi
about
(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)
and recalling the definition (
92
), (
94
) of
\bm{R}_{\rm even}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{\alpha,{\rm s},1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\partial_{s}\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)\,{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{s_{0}+\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert+\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}+\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\left\lvert Q_{\varphi,s}\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\right\rvert d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)\,{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert+\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\left\lvert Q_{\varphi,s}\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\right\rvert d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}}(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}}(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\widehat{s}\,\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\widehat{s}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\iint_{I_{1}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\iint_{I_{1}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2-\alpha}}\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\iint_{\rho\leq 4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\frac{1}{\rho^{2-\alpha}}\,\rho d\rho d\phi+\iint_{\rho\leq 5\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\frac{1}{\rho^{2-\alpha}}\,\rho d\rho d\phi\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,,

where we are using the notation
\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}
to denote
\bm{R}_{\rm even}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})
, and in the second-to-last line we have switched to polar coordinates as in the proof (
103
) of Lemma
3.4
.
Similarly, we may show that
J_{\alpha,\theta,1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{\alpha,\theta,1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\textstyle\left\lVert\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,.

Finally, we consider the integrals
J_{\alpha,{\rm s},2}
and
J_{\alpha,\theta,2}
over the region
I_{2}
. Note that
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}
belongs to the region
I_{2}^{\prime}
where
\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\theta}^{2}}>3\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}
.
Using the second expansion (
140
) of
\varphi
about
(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)
, we have that
J_{\alpha,{\rm s},2}
may be written

\displaystyle J_{\alpha,{\rm s},2}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)\iint_{I_{2}}\bigg(\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,\big(\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)\big)\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)\iint_{I_{2}}\bigg(\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert^{3}}+\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\bigg)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,,

where we are again using oddness in the middle line to insert
\bm{R}_{\rm even}
as in (
92
).
Then, expanding the differences
\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert^{3}}
and
\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}
using (
94
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{\alpha,{\rm s},2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\left\lvert\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}-\frac{\widehat{s}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}\right\rvert\,\big(\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{\alpha}+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{\alpha}\big)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg|\iint_{I_{2}}\bigg(\frac{\widehat{s}^{5}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{3}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert)}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{j}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even}\right\rvert^{2-j}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{5}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{4}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}(s_{0}+\widehat{s})^{3}\sin(\frac{\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert)}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{j}\left\lvert\bm{R}_{\rm even,0}\right\rvert^{2-j}}\bigg)\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3-\alpha}}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert^{2}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\quad+c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\alpha}}{\left\lvert\overline{\bm{R}}_{0}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}+\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\iint_{I_{2}}\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}_{0}\right\rvert^{3}}\,\epsilon d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\iint_{\rho>4\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}\bigg(\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert}{\rho^{3-\alpha}}+\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}}{\rho}+\frac{\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}}{\rho^{3}}\bigg)\,\rho d\rho d\phi

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg(\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert\big(1+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,-1+\alpha}\big)+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\big(1+\left\lvert\log(s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2})\right\rvert\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\big(1+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}\big)+\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}\big(1+\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,-1}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Here we have used the closeness of the kernels with respect to
s_{0}
and
\epsilon\theta_{0}
to obtain the second inequality, and have switched to polar coordinates in the third inequality.
Similarly, we may show that
J_{\alpha,\theta,2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{\alpha,\theta,2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

In total, we have

(141)

\left\lvert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\bigg(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\bigg)\,.

Combining (
137
) and (
141
), we obtain Lemma
4.3
.
∎

5.
Slender body NtD in Hölder spaces

In this section we prove Lemma
1.8
regarding Hölder space estimates for the slender body Neumann-to-Dirichlet map
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}
about a general curved filament.

Given a slender filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
as in section
1.1
with
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2,\alpha}
and slender body Neumann data
f(s)\in C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
,
we begin by considering the solution
u
to the slender body PDE (
8
) in
\Omega_{\epsilon}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\overline{\Sigma_{\epsilon}}
.
It will be useful to recall some results from the
L^{2}
-based solution theory for the slender body PDE
2
2

2

The solution theory in
[
36
]
is developed in the context of the Stokes equations; the analogous theory for the Laplace setting follows by nearly identical and somewhat simpler arguments.
developed in
[
36
]
. In particular, we recall the following bound (
[
36
, Theorem 1.2]
):

Lemma 5.1

(
L^{2}
-based SB PDE solution theory)
.

Given a slender body
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
as in section
1.1
with
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2}
and given
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
, the solution
u
to (
8
) belongs to

\displaystyle D^{1,2}(\Omega_{\epsilon}):=\{u\in L^{6}(\Omega_{\epsilon})\,:\,\nabla u\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})\}

and satisfies the estimate

(142)

\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{D^{1,2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}\equiv\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{1/2}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

For
r_{*}=r_{*}(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})<\frac{1}{2\kappa_{*}}
as in section
1.1
, we consider the curved annular region

(143)

\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}=\big\{\bm{x}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\;:\;\epsilon<\text{ dist}(\bm{x},\Gamma_{0})<r_{*}\big\}

about the filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
. Note that since
u
belongs to
L^{6}(\Omega_{\epsilon})
, the estimate (
142
) implies that
u
belongs to
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
and satisfies

(144)

\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\leq c(|\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}|)\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{D^{1,2}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\leq c(\kappa_{*},|\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}|)\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{1/2}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Within
\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
, we consider the weak form of the slender body PDE (
8
). Given
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
with
\phi\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=\phi(s)
and
\phi\big|_{\partial\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}\backslash\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=0
, the weak solution
u\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
to (
8
) satisfies

(145)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}}\nabla u\cdot\nabla\phi\,d\bm{x}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(s)\phi(s)\,ds\,.

For
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
, we may define a
C^{1,\beta}
change of variables
\Phi(\bm{x})
mapping the tube to a straight cylinder. In particular, for
\bm{x}=\bm{X}(s)+r\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
about the curved centerline
\bm{X}(s)
, we may take
\Phi(\bm{x})=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\cos\theta\bm{e}_{x}+r\sin\theta\bm{e}_{y}
where
(\bm{e}_{z},\bm{e}_{x},\bm{e}_{y})
are Cartesian basis vectors about a straight centerline.
We may calculate

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1-r\widehat{\kappa})\bm{e}_{\rm t}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+r\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)+\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)\,,

from which we may calculate

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi(\nabla\Phi)^{\rm T}\circ\Phi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+r^{2}\kappa_{3}^{2}}{(1-r\widehat{\kappa})^{2}}\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}-\frac{r\kappa_{3}}{1-r\widehat{\kappa}}\big(\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)+\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)\,.

Defining
{\bm{A}}(s,r,\theta)=\frac{1}{\left\lvert\det\nabla\Phi\right\rvert}\nabla\Phi(\nabla\Phi)^{\rm T}\circ\Phi^{-1}
, we note that within the region
\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
, we have
\frac{1}{c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})}<\left\lVert{\bm{A}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})
and
\bm{A}
depends smoothly on
\theta
.

Letting
\psi=\phi\circ\Phi^{-1}
and noting that for
\bm{y}\in\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
,
\psi(\bm{y})=\phi\big|_{\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}}
is independent of
\theta
, we may rewrite (
145
) in straightened coordinates
\bm{y}=\Phi(\bm{x})
as

(146)

\int_{\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\bm{A}\nabla u\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\bm{y}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(s)\psi(s)\,ds\,.

We will rely on the formulation (
146
) to prove Lemma
1.8
, but first introduce some auxiliary lemmas.

Following the construction of
[
19
, Chapter 5]
, we will build a Campanato-type function space designed for the slender body PDE.
For
\rho<r_{*}/2
, we define the following annular region within
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
about the straightened filament:

\displaystyle A_{\rho}(s_{0})

\displaystyle=\big\{\bm{y}=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\bm{e}_{r}+\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}\in\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})\;:\;s_{0}-\rho<s<s_{0}+\rho\,,\;\epsilon\leq r<\rho+\epsilon\,,\;0\leq\theta<2\pi\big\}\,.

Given a function
g(\bm{y})
,
\bm{y}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})
, let
g_{s_{0},\rho}
denote the mean of
g
over the annulus
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
:

\displaystyle g_{s_{0},\rho}=\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}g\,d\bm{y}\,.

We then define the Campanato-type seminorm
[\cdot]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}
and full norm
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}
by

(147)

\displaystyle{}_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{s_{0}\in\mathbb{T},\,0<\rho<r_{*}/2}\rho^{-\alpha}\bigg(\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{x}\bigg)^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}+[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,.

We note the following relationship between the space
\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}
and the Hölder space
C^{0,\alpha}
in the annular region
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
about the straightened filament.

Lemma 5.2

(Campanato-type norm bounds)
.

Given a function
g
in
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
satisfying
g\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=g(s)
, a function of arclength only, we have that

(148)

\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\leq c\,\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}\,.

Note that the
C^{0,\alpha}
norm on the left hand side is over the filament centerline (
s\in\mathbb{T}
), while the
C^{0,\alpha}
norm on the right hand side is over the entire annular region
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
.

Proof.

The second inequality is immediate: let
g\in C^{0,\alpha}(\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))
and consider
\bm{y},\bm{y}^{\prime}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})
. We have

\displaystyle\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g(\bm{y}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq c\,\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\rho^{\alpha}

for any choice of
\bm{y}^{\prime}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert\leq c\,\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\rho^{\alpha}\,,

and thus

\displaystyle\rho^{-\alpha}\bigg(\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Now consider
g\in\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}
and take
0<\rho<R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\lvert g_{s_{0},R}-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\leq 2\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}+2\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,.

Integrating over the smaller annulus
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
and noting that, for
\rho
small, the volume of the annulus scales as
\rho^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert g_{s_{0},R}-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}\bigg(\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g-g_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{x}+\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g-g_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{x}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}\big(R^{2\alpha+2}+\rho^{2\alpha+2}\big)[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}^{2}\leq c\frac{R^{2\alpha+2}}{\rho^{2}}[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}^{2}\,.

Defining
R_{k}=\frac{R}{2^{k}}
, we then note that

\displaystyle\left\lvert g_{s_{0},R_{k}}-g_{s_{0},R_{k+1}}\right\rvert\leq c\frac{R^{\alpha}}{2^{\alpha k}}[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,;

in particular, the sequence of annuli forms a Cauchy sequence limiting to the
s=s_{0}
cross section of the filament surface
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. Using that
g\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=g(s)
, by Lebesgue differentiation, the limit of the above sequence as
k\to\infty
is
g\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}(s_{0})
, with a rate

(149)

\left\lvert g_{s_{0},R}-g\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}(s_{0})\right\rvert\leq c\,R^{\alpha}[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,.

Now, for
\bm{y},\bm{y}^{\prime}\in\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
, write
\bm{y}=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}=s^{\prime}\bm{e}_{z}+r^{\prime}\bm{e}_{r}(\theta^{\prime})
. Let
R=\left\lvert s-s^{\prime}\right\rvert
be the centerline distance between the cross sections corresponding to
\bm{y}
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}
. For
R<\frac{r_{*}}{4}
, along the slender cylinder surface we may estimate

(150)

\left\lvert g(s_{0})-g(s_{0}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert g(s_{0})-g_{s_{0},2R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert g_{s_{0},2R}-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert+\left\lvert g(s_{0}^{\prime})-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert\,.

By the definition of
R
, we have
\left\lvert A_{2R}(s_{0})\cap A_{2R}(s_{0}^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\left\lvert A_{R}(s_{0})\right\rvert\geq cR^{2}
, since the volume of the annulus
A_{R}
scales like
R^{2}
when
R
is small. Thus, integrating the constant
\left\lvert g_{s_{0},2R}-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert
over
\left\lvert A_{2R}(s_{0})\cap A_{2R}(s_{0}^{\prime})\right\rvert
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert g_{s_{0},2R}-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left\lvert A_{2R}(s_{0})\cap A_{2R}(s_{0}^{\prime})\right\rvert}\bigg(\fint_{A_{2R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0},2R}\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}+\fint_{A_{2R}(s_{0}^{\prime})}\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{R^{2}}\bigg(R^{2}\bigg(\fint_{A_{2R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0},2R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\bigg)^{1/2}+R^{2}\bigg(\fint_{A_{2R}(s_{0}^{\prime})}\left\lvert g(\bm{y})-g_{s_{0}^{\prime},2R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\,R^{\alpha}[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,.

Combining this estimate with (
149
) in (
150
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert g(s_{0})-g(s_{0}^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lvert s_{0}-s_{0}^{\prime}\right\rvert^{\alpha}[g]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,.

∎

In addition to the Campanato-type characterization of Hölder continuity about
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, we will require a series of intermediary results regarding the following special version of slender body PDE. Let
R>0
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
and consider the annular region
A_{R}(s_{0})
about
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. Suppose
\bm{B}(\theta)
is a smooth, matrix-valued function supported in
A_{R}(s_{0})
depending only on the angle
\theta
and satisfying
\frac{1}{c}\leq\left\lvert\partial_{\theta}^{k}{\bm{B}}\right\rvert\leq c
for some
c>0
. Let
f_{0}
be a known constant and
d
be a given function on
\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
.
We consider the weak solution
h\in H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
to

(151)

\displaystyle-{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla h)

\displaystyle=0\qquad\quad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla h\cdot\bm{n}_{y}\,\epsilon\,d\theta

\displaystyle=f_{0}\qquad\;\,\text{on }\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle h\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle=h(s)\,,\quad\text{unknown but independent of }\theta

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=d\qquad\quad\text{on }\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}\,.

We have that
h
satisfies the following proposition.

Proposition 5.3

(Higher regularity for
h
)
.

Let
0<\rho<R
and consider the annulus
A_{\rho}(s_{0})\subset A_{R}(s_{0})
. The solution
h
to (
151
) satisfies

(152)

\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{4}(A_{\rho}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

Proof.

We consider the weak form of (
151
). For
g\in H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
with
g\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=g(s)
and
g\big|_{\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=0
, we have that a weak solution
h
to (
151
) satisfies

(153)

\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}{\bm{B}}\nabla h\cdot\nabla g\,d\bm{y}=f_{0}\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R}g(s)\,ds\,.

For some
R^{\prime}<R
, define a smooth cutoff function
\eta
supported up to the filament surface
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
and satisfying

(154)

\eta(\bm{x})=\begin{cases}1\,,&\bm{x}\in A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})\\
0\,,&\bm{x}\not\in A_{R}(s_{0})\,.\end{cases}

Within
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we consider the derivatives
\partial_{s}
,
\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}
in directions tangential to
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. We will take
\partial_{k}^{2}(\eta^{2}h)
,
\partial_{k}=\partial_{s},\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}
as our test function in (
153
) (this does not
a priori
belong to
H^{1}
but may be justified using finite differences).
Then for
\partial_{k}=\partial_{s},\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bm{B}\nabla h\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}^{2}(\eta^{2}h))\,d\bm{x}=f_{0}\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert<R}\partial_{k}^{2}(\eta^{2}h)\,ds=0\,.

Note that, in straight cylindrical coordinates,
\partial_{s}
commutes with
\nabla
while
\left\lvert[\nabla,\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}]g\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\lvert\nabla g\right\rvert
.
Since
\bm{B}(\theta)
is bounded below, after commuting and integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}h\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\eta\big(\left\lvert\bm{B}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{B}\right\rvert\big)\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}h\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\bm{B}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert\big(\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}^{2}\eta^{2}\right\rvert\left\lvert h\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla^{2}\eta^{2}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}\,.

Using Young’s inequality on the right hand side, over the smaller set
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
, we may bound

(155)

\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{k}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,,\qquad\partial_{k}=\partial_{s},\textstyle\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\,.

We then write out

(156)
