{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla h)=\frac{1}{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\bigg(r(\bm{B}\nabla h)\cdot\bm{e}_{r}\bigg)+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigg((\bm{B}\nabla h)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}\bigg)+\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\bigg((\bm{B}\nabla h)\cdot\bm{e}_{z}\bigg)\,,

where
\nabla h=\partial_{r}h\bm{e}_{r}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}h\bm{e}_{\theta}+\partial_{s}h\bm{e}_{z}
and
\left\lvert\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\bm{B}(\theta)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{\epsilon}
. We may then combine the tangential regularity estimate (
155
) with the fact that
h
satisfies
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla h)=0
in
A_{R^{\prime}}
to bound second derivatives in directions normal to the filament surface:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{rr}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\left\lVert\nabla h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}+\textstyle\left\lVert\nabla(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}h)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\bigg)\,.

In total, we obtain an
H^{2}
bound for
h
over the region
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
:

(157)

\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

We may iterate this procedure over slightly smaller annuli. Within
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
, using that
\partial_{s}
commutes with
{\rm{div}\,}
and
\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla
, we have that
\partial_{s}h
(weakly) satisfies

(158)

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla\partial_{s}h)

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{in }A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}h)\cdot\bm{n}_{y}\,\epsilon\,d\theta

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{on }\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\partial_{s}h\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}h(s)\,,\quad\text{independent of }\theta\,.

Furthermore, we have that
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}h
satisfies

(159)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}h)

\displaystyle=\textstyle[{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla),\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}]h\quad\text{in }A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}h\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Here we note that the right hand side commutator satisfies

(160)

\textstyle\left\lVert[{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla),\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}]h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\,.

We consider the weak form of (
158
) and (
159
) as in (
153
), this time with test function
g=\partial_{\ell}^{2}\eta_{2}^{2}(\partial_{k}h)
,
\partial_{\ell},\partial_{k}\in\{\partial_{s},\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\}
where
\eta_{2}
is a smooth cutoff satisfying, for
R^{\prime\prime}<R^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\eta_{2}(\bm{x})=\begin{cases}1&\bm{x}\in A_{R^{\prime\prime}}(s_{0})\\
0&\bm{x}\not\in A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}).\end{cases}

Again, this
g
does not
a priori
belong to
H^{1}
but may be justified using finite differences. After a series of integration by parts we obtain a similar bound to (
155
):

(161)

\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{\ell}\partial_{k}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\bigg)\,.

To obtain an
H^{3}
estimate for the normal direction to the straightened slender body, we note that within
A_{R^{\prime\prime}}
,
h
satisfies

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla\partial_{r}h)

\displaystyle=[{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla),\partial_{r}]h\,,

where
[{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla),\partial_{r}]h
also satisfies (
160
). Using (
156
), we have that
\partial_{rrr}h
may be written entirely in terms of functions which belong to
L^{2}
, and may thus be bounded as

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{rrr}h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}^{2}\nabla h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}})}+\left\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}\nabla h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}})}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\partial_{s}\nabla h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}})}+\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\bigg)\,.

In total, using (
157
), we obtain the full
H^{3}
bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{3}(A_{R^{\prime\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}\,.

We may perform this entire procedure one more time to obtain, for
R^{\prime\prime\prime}<R^{\prime\prime}<R^{\prime}<R
, the bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{4}(A_{R^{\prime\prime\prime}}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

∎

Using Proposition
5.3
, we may show that
h
satisfies the following.

Corollary 5.4

(Caccioppoli inequality)
.

For
0<R^{\prime}<R
, the solution
h
to (
151
) satisfies

(162)

\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon)\frac{1}{(R-R^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Proof.

We have that
\partial_{s}h
satisfies (
158
) within
A_{R}(s_{0})
for some
R>0
. Furthermore,
\partial_{s}h-\lambda
also satisfies (
158
) for any constant
\lambda
since
(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}
is still independent of
\theta
. Let
\eta
be a cutoff function as in (
154
). Multiplying (
158
) by
\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)
and integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\cdot\nabla(\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}h-\lambda))\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\cdot\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\,d\bm{y}+2\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta\bm{B}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\cdot\nabla\eta\,(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\,d\bm{y}\,.

Therefore, using that
\bm{B}
is bounded below, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\eta\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{B}\right\rvert\left\lvert\eta\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-\lambda\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}\,.

Using Young’s inequality and that
\eta=1
on
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
while
\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\sim\frac{1}{R-R^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla(\partial_{s}h-\lambda)\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq\frac{c}{(R-R^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-\lambda\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Taking
\lambda=(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},R}
, we obtain (
162
).

∎

Using Proposition
5.3
and Corollary
5.4
, we may prove the following rate-dependent estimate for the solution
h
to (
151
).

Lemma 5.5
.

For
0<\rho<R/2
, the solution
h
to (
151
) satisfies

(163)

\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Proof.

Using a Poincaré inequality, Sobolev embedding, and Corollary
5.4
, we have that
h
satisfies

\displaystyle\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R/2}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rvert^{2}\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\fint_{A_{3R/4}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}h\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

∎

Given Lemmas
5.2
and
5.5
, we may proceed to the proof of Lemma
1.8
.

Proof of Lemma
1.8
.

We consider the weak solution
u
to the slender body PDE, which satisfies (
146
) in the straightened region
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
. For
0<R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, we consider the annular region
A_{R}(s_{0})
within
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
.

Within
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we write
u=h+q
where
h
satisfies the following version of the slender body PDE with frozen coefficients in
s
and
r
. Given the matrix
\bm{A}(s,r,\theta)
, let
\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)=\bm{A}(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
. We consider
h
satisfying

(164)

\displaystyle-{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{A}_{0}\nabla h)

\displaystyle=0\qquad\quad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{A}_{0}\nabla h\cdot\bm{n}_{y}\,\epsilon d\theta

\displaystyle=f(s_{0})\quad\;\text{on }\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})

\displaystyle h\big|_{\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})}

\displaystyle=h(s)\,,\quad\text{unknown but independent of }\theta

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=u\qquad\quad\text{on }\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})\,.

Since
\bm{A}_{0}
depends smoothly on
\theta
, we may use Lemma
5.5
to obtain a bound for the oscillation of
\partial_{s}h
for any
\rho<R/2
:

(165)

\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}d\bm{y}\,.

Furthermore, rewriting (
146
) as

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}(\bm{A}-\bm{A}_{0})\nabla u\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\bm{y}+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bm{A}_{0}(\nabla h+\nabla q)\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R}f(s)\psi(s)\,ds\,,

we may use the weak form of (
164
) to obtain an equation for
\nabla q
:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bm{A}_{0}\nabla q\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=-\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}(\bm{A}-\bm{A}_{0})\nabla u\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\bm{y}+\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R}\big(f(s)-f(s_{0})\big)\psi(s)\,ds\,.

Since
q=0
on
\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
and
q=q(s)
on
\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
by construction, using that the matrix
\bm{A}_{0}
is bounded below, we may estimate

(166)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\bigg(R^{\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(A_{R})}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\left\lVert\nabla q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}

\displaystyle+R^{1/2+\alpha}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert q\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert<R)}\bigg)\,.

Note that, by a scaling argument,
q|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}
satisfies the following trace inequality within the annulus
A_{R}(s_{0})
:

\displaystyle\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert<R}\left\lvert q\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert<R}\left\lvert q\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rvert^{2}\,dsd\theta\leq c(\epsilon)\,R\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}+R^{-2}\left\lvert q\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\,R\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Using Young’s inequality and the definition of
\bm{A}
, the estimate (
166
) yields

(167)

\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(R^{2\alpha}\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+R^{2+2\alpha}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\,.

Furthermore, by Proposition
5.3
, we have that, for
\rho<R/2
, the function
h
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert^{2}\leq c\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R/2}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla h\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,R)\rho^{2}\left\lVert\nabla h\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\left\lVert\nabla h\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}^{2}\,,

by scaling. From this we may obtain

(168)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon)\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla q\right\rVert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}+R^{2\alpha}\bigg)\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+R^{2+2\alpha}\,c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\,,

where we have used the bound (
167
).

We now state a useful result from
[
19
, Lemma 5.13]
.

Proposition 5.6

(Lemma 5.13 from
[
19
]
)
.

Given a nondecreasing function
\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfying

\displaystyle\Psi(\rho)\leq c_{1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{m}+\omega\bigg)\Psi(R)+c_{2}R^{\nu}\,,\quad m>\nu>0

for all
0<\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
, for
\omega
sufficiently small (depending on
c_{1},m,\nu
), we in fact have

(169)

\Psi(\rho)\leq c_{3}\bigg(\frac{\Psi(R)}{R^{\nu}}+c_{2}\bigg)\rho^{\nu}

for all
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
.

Applying Proposition
5.6
to (
168
) with
\Psi(\rho)=\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}
,
m=2
, and
\nu=2-\delta
for small
\delta>0
, we obtain

(170)

\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(R^{-(2-\delta)}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+R^{2\alpha+\delta}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\rho^{2-\delta}

for any
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
.

Using (
165
) and (
167
), for
0<\rho\leq\widetilde{\rho}\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
we may then estimate

(171)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq 2\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}d\bm{y}+2\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{\widetilde{\rho}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}h-(\partial_{s}h)_{s_{0},\widetilde{\rho}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+4\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{\widetilde{\rho}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\widetilde{\rho}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla q\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{\widetilde{\rho}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\widetilde{\rho}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(\widetilde{\rho}^{2\alpha}\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\widetilde{\rho}^{2+2\alpha}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{\widetilde{\rho}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{\widetilde{\rho}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\widetilde{\rho}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\,\widetilde{\rho}^{2+2\alpha-\delta}\,\widetilde{C}\,,

where
\widetilde{C}=R^{-(2-\delta)}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+R^{\delta}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}
.

By Proposition
5.6
with
\Psi(\rho)=\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}
,
m=4
, and
\nu=2+2\alpha-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2+2\alpha-\delta}\,c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(R^{-(2+2\alpha-\delta)}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\widetilde{C}\bigg)

for any
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
.
By Lemma
5.2
, we thus have
\partial_{s}u\big|_{\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})\cap\partial A_{R}(s_{0})}\in C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}
for small
\delta>0
. Since
u\big|_{\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})}
is independent of
\theta
, we also have
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}u\big|_{\Phi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})\cap\partial A_{R}(s_{0})}\in C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}
. Thus by classical elliptic regularity theory we have
u\in C^{1,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
throughout the annulus
A_{R}(s_{0})
with

(172)

\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\big(\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\big)\,.

In particular,
\nabla u\in L^{\infty}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
, and we may estimate

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c\,R^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}\,.

We may then improve the estimate (
171
) to

(173)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\,R^{2+2\alpha}\,\widetilde{C}_{2}\,,

where
\widetilde{C}_{2}=\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla u\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}^{2}
. Again applying Proposition
5.6
, we have

(174)

\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq\rho^{2\alpha}\,c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\alpha})\bigg(R^{-2\alpha}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}u-(\partial_{s}u)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\widetilde{C}_{2}\bigg)\,.

Since we may cover the region
\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
by annuli
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we thus obtain
\partial_{s}u\in\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}
, and therefore, by Lemma
5.2
,
u=u(s)\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
along the filament surface
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
. The estimate (
42
) follows from the bounds (
172
) and (
144
) as well as Lemma
5.2
.

∎
